whether they are notable or not—nonetheless, the **Spirit of Life** <term>spiritus vitæ</term> <note>Latin: *spiritus vitæ* — the vital breath or force that animates the body</note> of them all is the single spirit and the single **power** <term>virtus</term> <note>Latin: *virtus* — inherent strength or medicinal quality</note> and **working** <term>operationn</term> <note>original: *operationn* — the functional activity of the spirit</note>.

## The Second Chapter.

The origin and the descent of the **Spirit of Life** <term>spiritus vitæ</term> is derived from **external generations** <term>externis generationibus</term> <note>Latin: *externis generationibus* — here implying that the spirit has a source outside of mere physical reproduction</note>, and not **naturally according to the flesh** <term>naturaliter iuxta carnem</term> <note>Latin: *naturaliter iuxta carnem* — the author argues the spirit is not a byproduct of the physical body's growth</note>; for **generation** <term>generatio</term> <note>Latin: *generatio* — the process of being born or created</note> proceeds in a twofold way, yet the birth of the spirit is only simple. It is united with the body and its limbs as has been mentioned; therefore, it cannot be made firm in its own strength. Rather, the limbs themselves must be firm; and the firmer, healthier, and more robust they are by its nature, the stronger the spirit is also. Therefore, we should by no means attempt to preserve, strengthen, or increase the **Spirit of Life** <term>spiritum vitæ</term> in any other way than solely by ensuring that the limbs—which have flowed out of nature—are rectified in their primary **generation** <term>generation</term>.

<summary>This page explains that the Spirit of Life is a single, unified force with an "external" origin rather than a physical one. It emphasizes that to strengthen one's vital spirit, one must focus on the health and "rectification" of the physical limbs and organs through which it acts.</summary>
<keywords>Spirit of Life, vital force, generation, physiology, bodily health, Paracelsian medicine</keywords>

--- Page 353 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion on the preservation of the limbs to support the Spirit of Life. The opening "gen" likely completes the word "rectify" or "purify" from the preceding page's instruction on limb health.</meta>

...ify and preserve them in their right essence; then that same spirit strengthens itself. But there is one thing which belongs to the spirit alone: that it remains open in its **passages** <term>meatibus</term> <note>Latin: *meatus* — the anatomical channels or pores through which the vital spirits were thought to flow</note> and is **cleared of blockages** <term>deoppilirt</term> <note>from the Latin *deoppilatus* — a medical term meaning to remove obstructions or "oppilations" within the body's vessels</note>. Regarding this clearing, and how such openings are made, we shall provide a specific **treatise** <term>Libell</term> <note>Germanized Latin: *Libell* — a small book or dedicated chapter on a specific subject</note>.

Following that, regarding the limbs which must rectify themselves, two treatises follow hereafter. From this it is important to note that the **Spirit of Life** <term>spiritus vitæ</term> can be obstructed, even to the point of being completely suffocated, if its paths are not open, along with the **pores** <term>pori</term> of its **passages** <term>meatuum</term>.

For the **second generation** <term>secunda generatio</term> <note>In early modern medical philosophy, this refers to a spiritual or alchemical "rebirth" or refinement of substances within the body, distinct from physical birth</note> is nothing other than a vapor; and in the form of a vapor, the **Spirit of Life** <term>spiritus vitæ</term> lives within us according to the nature of the **second generation** <term>secundæ generationis</term>, which occurs outside of nature. Therefore, above all things, the pores should initially be kept open in the **marrow** <term>medullen</term>, in the **flesh** <term>carne</term>, in the **veins** <term>venis</term>, in the **principal organs** <term>membris principalibus</term>, and in the **bones** <term>ossibus</term>,

<summary>The author explains that the Spirit of Life acts like a vapor that requires unobstructed bodily passages to function, introducing the concept of "second generation" as a non-natural process of spiritual maintenance. He lists the specific parts of the body—from marrow to bone—where these pores must remain open to prevent the spirit from suffocating.</summary>
<keywords>Spirit of Life, deoppilation, second generation, anatomy, medical treatises, vital vapor, bodily passages</keywords>

...in the **joints** <term>iuncturis</term>, in the **bladder** <term>vesica</term>, in the **diaphragm** <term>diaphragmate</term>, in the **womb** <term>matrice</term>, etc. And likewise in all members of the whole body, none excepted: For it <note>the Spirit of Life</note> lies in the **brain** <term>cerebro</term> just as in the **flesh** <term>carne</term>, and passes through the brain back and forth, out and in, through the **delicate** <term>piam matrem</term> and **tough** <term>duram matrem</term> **membranes** <note>Latin: *pia mater* and *dura mater*—the inner and outer protective layers surrounding the brain</note>. It also passes out of and into the **heart** <term>cor</term>; likewise the **gallbladder** <term>cystam fellis</term>; also through the **regions of the kidneys** <term>regiones renum</term> into the center of the **bladder** <term>vesicae</term>; it occupies both the empty and the full.

## The Third Chapter.

Now take note of this: wherever the **Spirit of Life** <term>spiritus vitæ</term> cannot reach, a disease is born there. Namely, if it is blocked and barred, then **decay** <term>putrefactio</term> and **ulceration** <term>ulceratio</term> occur there; for it dies in that very place, and what is dead always follows: it rots afterward and creates an **ulcer** <term>ulcus</term> according to the nature and matter of its own limb. Thus, in the **principal organs** <term>principalibus</term> <note>the vital organs such as the heart, brain, and liver</note>, it leads to death, and thus from **obstruction** <term>opilatio</term>... <note>the text cuts off mid-word, likely completing as 'opilatione' on the following page</note>

<summary>This page describes the Spirit of Life's pervasive movement through all organs and brain membranes, warning that any blockage of this flow causes tissue death, decay, and disease.</summary>
<keywords>Spirit of Life, anatomy, brain membranes, vital organs, pathology, blockages, ulceration, putrefaction</keywords>

...from the **obstruction** <term>opilatione</term> of the **Spirit of Life** <term>spiritus vitæ</term> arise **fevers** <term>febres</term>, **abscesses** <term>apostemata</term>, **pleurisy** <term>pleurisis</term>, **jaundice** <term>ictericia</term>, **ulcers** <term>vlcera</term>, and similar things that begin with a **feverish attack** <term>paroxysmo febrili</term>, such as the **plague** <term>pestis</term> and others. 

The cause and the sign are as follows: When the spirit is meant to suffocate and die, it shudders through the entire body at that same location—first with **chill** <note>original German: *frost*</note>, because it initially **coagulates** <note>it thickens or clumps together</note>. Afterward, it follows with **heat**, once it has **dissolved** <note>original Latin: *resoluirt*, meaning the blockage has been broken down or liquefied</note>; except only for those **fevers** <term>febres</term> that recur more than once, such as the **daily** <term>quotidiana</term>, the **tertian** <term>tertiana</term> <note>occurring every three days</note>, the **quartan** <term>quartana</term> <note>occurring every four days</note>, etc. 

Thus, when it <note>the Spirit of Life</note> suffers an attack in the **brain** <term>cerebro</term>, it causes **frenzy** <term>phrenesim</term> <note>inflammation or delirium</note>, **mania**, and the like. But if it suffers an attack in the **heart** <term>corde</term>, it causes **Saint Vitus’s Dance** <term>morbum sancti Viti</term> <note>a disorder characterized by rapid, uncoordinated jerking movements</note>, for it wounds the **veins of joy** <term>venas lætitię</term> <note>historical anatomical concept of vessels thought to carry the spirits of cheerfulness</note>; and it happens so often that it strikes according to the **time, hour, and year**; this indicates a **paroxysm of the caverns** <term>paroxysmum cauernarum</term> <note>a recurring medical crisis rooted in the internal cavities of the body</note>. As for the reasons why this happens, and especially in **fevers** and **pe**... <note>the text cuts off mid-word, likely 'pestis' or 'plague'</note>

<summary>This page explains how the suffocation and coagulation of the Spirit of Life leads to various ailments, including fevers, mania, and neurological disorders like Saint Vitus's Dance. It details the physical sensations of these "attacks," transitioning from chills to heat as the vital spirit reacts to blockages.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion on the nature of the Spirit of Life, completing a sentence from the previous page about how its nature should be understood.</meta>

...you should understand it in this way: The **Spirit of Life** <term>spiritus vitæ</term> <note>the vital force or breath that animates the body</note> is subject to celestial **influence** <term>influenz</term> <note>referring to the "in-flowing" of power from the stars and heavens</note>, in the same way that the body is subject to the earth. And just as the body "eats" the earth—that is, when it eats and must sustain itself from the earth—so also does the **Spirit of Life** eat from the higher **influence** and nourishes itself from the element of **Fire** <term>Ignis</term>. In this, the four elements **shine forth** <note>original: *refulgiren*, meaning to reflect or radiate</note>, just as the earth in its fruit shines forth in the four elements; so also here the **influence** shines forth. Therefore, two **practical applications** <term>Practica</term> are undertaken here: the first is that the **influence** be initially maintained, insofar as it is pleasing and belongs to the spirit; the second is that the body be maintained as subject to the spirit. From this then follows the health of the body in a certain way, as far as it concerns the spirit.

Although my nature still remains the same, the effect is no longer present. In this way, a pious man often becomes a thief under an evil **influence**, as we have further written concerning the **external senses of men** <note>original: *de externis sensibus hominum*</note>.

## The Fourth Chapter /
### On the powers of the spleen. <note>original Latin: *De viribus splenis*</note>

The spleen is like the other organs; therefore, it is necessary that it be well understood, just as much as the other members. Its corruption lies in the fact that it develops abscesses <note>original: *apostemiert*</note> through various fevers, decay <term>putrefaction</term> <note>original: *putrefactio*</note>, and blockages <term>obstructions</term> <note>original: *opilatio*</note>, and indeed many more like these. Its healing power consists in this: that the physical spleen is removed and the "external spleen" is put in its place. For life is easier without a spleen than with one. Although the aforementioned auxiliary organs <note>original: *beyglid*; referring to other vital organs like the heart or liver</note> do not allow for this—rather, they must remain in the body—it is not so with the spleen <note>original: *splen*</note>; instead, we can live quite well without it. This is a matter upon which further medical practice must be based.

<summary>This page concludes the discussion on liver-related ailments and introduces Chapter 4, which argues that the spleen is unique because, unlike other vital organs, a person can live without its physical form by relying on its external medicinal essence.</summary>
<keywords>liver, spleen, dropsy, jaundice, fevers, abscesses, putrefaction, obstructions, spiritual organs, medical practice</keywords>

## The Sixth Chapter
->On the powers of the gall<- <note>original Latin: *De viribus fellis*; referring to the gallbladder and the bile it contains</note>

The highest power and the most necessary thing is that we take away and completely consume the **gall**, just as it physically lies in the body. In doing so, the **choleric illnesses** <note>original Latin: *colericæ ægritudines*; diseases traditionally attributed to an excess or corruption of yellow bile</note> will subsequently be avoided, such as **colic** <note>original: *colica*</note>, **iliac passion** <note>original: *iliaca*; a severe intestinal obstruction or painful abdominal condition</note>, **contraction** <term>contractura</term> <note>original: *contractura*; a permanent tightening or shortening of muscles or joints</note>, **tremors** <note>original: *tremor*</note>, and others of the same kind. Therefore, one can well do without and dispense with them [the internal gall], for these members are of no use, except only to destroy <note>original: *erbrechen*; here used in the sense of breaking down or undoing</note> that which the healthy members build up. For this reason, the so-called **physicians** <note>original: *physici*</note> have written wrongly until now; indeed, they not only failed to realize that the gall is useless, but some even gave it great praise, though it is not worthy of any praise at all.

### Chapter Six

The liquid *Alchahest* <term>Alchahest</term> <note>original: *liquor Alchahest*; a hypothetical "universal solvent" in alchemy, famously sought by Paracelsus and his followers for its ability to dissolve all substances and cure all ailments</note> is also of great power in the liver, to preserve it from dropsy <note>original: *wassersucht*; a historical medical term for edema or swelling caused by fluid accumulation, frequently associated with liver disease</note> and all related conditions that arise from the liver. Its process consists in this: that after its solidification <note>original: *congelation*</note>, it is dissolved <note>original: *resoluirt*</note>, and then coagulated into a transformed state, as its process demonstrates through freezing and dissolving <note>original: *per congelationem et resolutionem*</note>. Then, when it overcomes its like <note>likely referring to the medicine surpassing the specific nature of the disease or matching the patient's constitution</note>, it is the medicine for the liver above all other medicines. And even if the liver were already failing <note>original: *zerbrochen*; literally "broken," suggesting severe damage or cirrhosis</note>, this medicine restores the whole liver, just as if it had never been damaged. Therefore, it is highly necessary for you physicians to master this medicine, so that you know how to prepare *Alchahest*, because of the many diseases that [arise] from...

# The Seventh Chapter
## Regarding the Spleen

Regarding the spleen <term>spleen</term> <note>original: *miltz*; in the medical tradition of this era, the spleen was believed to be the seat of "black bile" and melancholy, and was often treated for obstructions that caused various systemic illnesses</note>, to completely expel and remove its [afflictions], this is accomplished through Tartar <term>Tartar</term> <note>original: *Tartarum*; the potassium bitartrate sediment found in wine casks. In Paracelsian medicine, "Tartaric" diseases were those involving stony deposits, blockages, or calcification within the organs</note> in this manner: you must separate its liquid <note>original: *liquorem*</note> from its corrosive quality <note>original: *corrosiff*</note> using a retort <term>retort</term> <note>original: *per retortam*; a glass vessel with a long, downward-pointing neck used for distilling substances</note> over a powerful fire. Afterward, distill it again five times, then cohobate <term>cohobate</term> <note>original: *cohobirs*; an alchemical process where the distilled liquid is poured back onto the solid remains (the "head") and re-distilled to increase its potency</note> it, and finally rectify it with "wine of life" <note>original: *vino vitæ*; a term for highly concentrated or quintessentially distilled alcohol, often called *aqua vitae*</note>. When used, it carries away the [malady of the] spleen, and it vanishes entirely, leaving no trace of the spleen's [obstruction] there. It also removes that which the spleen was meant to digest <note>this refers to the organ's perceived role in processing and "cleansing" the blood of heavy humors</note> and cleanses the body of all its related ailments <note>original: *züfählen*; secondary symptoms or accompanying conditions</note>. Furthermore, this same medicinal power is also to be sought in the dregs <note>original: *fecibus*; the solid residue remaining after distillation</note> which have been separated out by the wine of life.

<summary>This page introduces alchemical treatments for the spleen using Tartar. It details a specific preparation process involving repeated distillation and cohobation to create a remedy that clears organ obstructions and purifies the body.</summary>

<keywords>spleen, Tartar, distillation, cohobation, wine of life, alchemical medicine, organ cleansing, potassium bitartrate</keywords>

## The Sixth Chapter

The liquid Alchahest <term>Alchahest</term> <note>original: *liquor Alchahest*; in Paracelsian medicine, the Alkahest was a hypothetical "universal solvent" capable of dissolving any substance and acting as a powerful medicine for the internal organs</note> is also of great power in the liver, to protect the same from dropsy <term>dropsy</term> <note>original: *wassersucht*; an old medical term for edema or the swelling caused by the accumulation of excess fluid in the body's tissues, often linked to liver failure</note> and all its varieties that come from the liver. And its process consists in this: that after its congelation <term>congelation</term> <note>original: *congelationē*; the alchemical process of cooling or thickening a substance into a solid or semi-solid state</note> it is resolved <term>resolved</term> <note>original: *resoluirt*; the process of dissolving a solid back into a liquid state</note>, and then coagulated into a transmuted form, as its process demonstrates through congelation and resolution <note>original: *per congelationē et resolutionem*</note>. Then, when it overcomes its like <note>this refers to the alchemical principle where the purified medicine "conquers" the diseased state of the same nature</note>, it is the medicine for the liver beyond all other medicines. Even if the liver were already broken <note>original: *zerbrochen*; implying severe damage, decay, or loss of function</note>, it [the medicine] replaces the whole liver as if it had never been damaged. Therefore, it is of great necessity for you physicians to learn of this medicine, so that you know how to prepare <term>prepare</term> <note>original: *præpariren*; the chemical processing and refining of a raw substance into a medicinal form</note> Alchahest, because of the many illnesses which [arise] from...

# The Seventh Chapter
## Of the Spleen.

Regarding the spleen, to drive it away entirely and to take it away / that is done through **Tartar** <term>Tartar</term> <note>original: *Tartarum*; in Paracelsian medicine, "tartar" refers to the stony precipitates found in wine casks, which were believed to cause "tartaric" diseases like kidney stones and gout when they formed inside the human body. Once purified alchemically, however, it became a potent remedy.</note> / in this way / that you separate from it its **liquid** <term>liquid</term> <note>original: *liquorem*</note> without its **corrosive** <term>corrosive</term> <note>original: *corrosiff*; an acidic or caustic substance</note> / **by means of a retort** <term>retort</term> <note>original: *per retortam*; a glass vessel with a long, downward-pointing neck used for distilling liquids</note> with powerful fire / afterward draw it off again five times / then **cohobate** <term>cohobate</term> <note>original: *cohobirs*; an alchemical term for repeating the distillation of a liquid by pouring it back onto the solid residue left in the vessel</note> and then **rectify** <term>rectify</term> <note>original: *rectificire*; the process of refining a substance, usually through repeated distillation, to reach its purest state</note> with the **wine of life** <term>wine of life</term> <note>original: *vino vitæ*; likely a reference to high-proof distilled alcohol or aqua vitæ, used as a medicinal solvent</note> and use it / it takes away the spleen / and it goes away entirely / leaving no trace of the spleen there / <note>The text suggests "taking away" the spleen, which in a Paracelsian context often refers to removing the diseased "mortal" part or the obstruction within the organ rather than surgical removal.</note> And it also takes away that which the spleen should **digest** <term>digest</term> <note>original: *digerieren*; to break down or process through heat or biological action</note> / [and] cleanses the body of all its afflictions / And so also in the **dregs** <term>dregs</term> <note>original: *fecibus*; the solid sediment or waste left after a liquid has been filtered or distilled</note> therefrom / which are separated by the wine of life / that same power is to be sought.

## The Eighth Chapter.

Similarly, in **Tamarisk** <term>Tamarisk</term> <note>original: *Tamarise*; a flowering shrub or small tree whose bark and leaves were historically used in medicine to treat obstructions of the spleen.</note> such a virtue is to be sought / and is found / when it is steeped in **olive oil** <note>original: *oleo oliuarum*</note> / then mixed with **opopanax** <term>opopanax</term> <note>original: *oppopanaco*; a gummy resin obtained from the herb *Opopanax chironium*, used in traditional medicine as an antispasmodic.</note> / and distilled **by means of an alembic** <term>alembic</term> <note>original: *alembicum*; a classic alchemical distillation apparatus consisting of two vessels connected by a tube.</note> / this same oil, when rubbed onto the site of the **spleen** <note>original: *miltz*</note> / takes the spleen entirely away, root and all / and if it is used **annually** <note>original: *annuatim*</note> / then it averts and drives away all pains / that come from the spleen / But when it is prepared according to the true foundation / it takes the spleen away entirely / and consumes it / and all its damages / pains / illnesses / and whatever **misfortune** <note>original: *vnfahl*</note> it causes.

### The Eleventh Chapter /
### Concerning the Lungs.
